# P3: Hooks (Claude and Codex) implementation plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make Claude Code and Codex sessions first-class in acd. Claude
`http` hooks and the `acd hook codex` shim feed a hook endpoint in acd.
Claude permission prompts are held and answered through the hook reply.
Questions, Codex approvals, and Claude prompts whose hook has ended are
answered with guarded keys through P2. Sessions are bound to herdr panes,
migrate when a binding appears, and yield to T3 when T3 owns them.
`acd hooks install|uninstall`, hook-token rotation and `acd service
install` ship with it, plus every P3 obligation from the P1 follow-ups.

**Architecture:** A new `hooks` module in `crates/acd-adapters`. An axum
router (`hooks/endpoint.rs`) authenticates `POST /hook/<agent>/<event>`,
replies to lifecycle hooks at once, and parks Claude `PermissionRequest`
connections as **holds**. Every inbound hook goes into one unbounded
mailbox. One **hooks reconciler task** (`hooks/state.rs`) drains it in
arrival order and is the only code that upserts hooks-only agents and
opens, migrates or ends hook requests. It never awaits herdr: screen
reads run in spawned tasks that post their results back. `HooksAdapter`
implements `acd_core::Adapter` for `AnswerPath::Hook` and `Source::Hooks`.
Its `deliver` writes the reply body into the hold. Keys answers stay in
P2's engine; P3 registers sealed `KeysTarget`s and supplies the
`Evidence` implementation (`hooks/evidence.rs`). The `acd` binary gains
the shim, the installer, rotation, the service installer, and the
listener wiring. Core gains agent migration, a pending-key lookup and a
resolution `reason`.

**Tech Stack:** Rust (edition 2024, rust-version 1.90), tokio, axum 0.8
(hook listener, now a normal dependency of `acd-adapters`), serde_json
with the `raw_value` feature (order-preserving settings edits), sha2,
chrono (registry `procStart` parsing), libc (process start time),
acd-securefs (no-follow reads). The shim uses only `std::net`. Tests use
FakeHerdr, the core `testing` feature, a raw-TCP hook client, and temp
`HOME` directories.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-agent-inbox-design.md`:
§4 (invariants 1 to 4), §5 and §5.1 (keys, occurrences, attribution,
upgrade, exclusive answer paths, agent rules), §6 (adapter contract),
§6.1 (endpoint, installation, token storage, rotation, coexisting
hooks), §6.2 (Claude), §6.3 (Codex), §6.4 (herdr fallback, follow-ups),
§7.1 (tokens, logs), §9 (config and commands), §11 gates 2, 4, 5 and 7,
§12, §13 and §14 items 1, 2, 4, 6, 8 and 9. Evidence:
`docs/research/2026-10-07-p0-checks.md`, the draft gate results
`docs/research/2026-10-09-p3-gates-claude.md` (gates 2, 5, 7) and
`docs/research/2026-10-09-p3-gate4-codex.md` (gate 4), and the fixtures
in `tests/fixtures/hooks/{claude,codex}/` and `tests/fixtures/herdr/`.
Obligations: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-p1-followups.md`,
section "P3: hooks and service" (mapped one by one below).

## Pending gate results

The gate checks were still running when this plan was started. Two draft
result documents appeared while it was being written
(`docs/research/2026-10-09-p3-gates-claude.md`,
`docs/research/2026-10-09-p3-gate4-codex.md`). The plan follows those
drafts where they are explicit and the spec where they are silent. Each
item below names the assumption, where the plan depends on it, and what
result would change it. The orchestrator should confirm or amend each
one and fold the accepted ones into the spec (Task 20 does the spec
edit). Tasks also read fixtures those gate agents added; confirm they
are final before execution: `tests/fixtures/hooks/claude/`
`session-registry-entry.json`, `session-registry-status-sequence.jsonl`,
`payload-Stop-after-deny.json` and `settings-http-hooks-env-token.json`,
and `tests/fixtures/herdr/agent-info-codex-*.json` and
`agent-list-codex-fresh-app-server-misbound.json`.

1. **PENDING GATE 2: header env interpolation (2a).** Draft: PASS.
   `$VAR` in a header value is replaced when the variable is listed in
   `allowedEnvVars`; an unlisted variable becomes `""`. Hook config is a
   snapshot taken at session start and re-read only on `/clear`, resume
   or restart. Plan: Claude entries carry `X-Herdr-Pane:
   $HERDR_PANE_ID`, `X-Herdr-Socket: $HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`,
   `X-Claude-Entrypoint: $CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`, and the literal token
   (Task 16a). The endpoint treats an empty header as absent (Task 7).
   Changes it: if interpolation fails, drop the herdr headers and bind
   Claude sessions only through herdr `agent_session.value` (rule 2,
   second clause, already implemented in Task 9).
2. **PENDING GATE 2: held connection on a terminal answer (2b).** Draft:
   PARTIAL. The connection closes within about 15 ms on terminal deny or
   Esc, and **stays open on terminal allow** until the hook timeout.
   Plan (Task 11c): a hold close means deny, Esc, interruption or process
   death, and follows spec §6.2 step 4. A terminal allow is detected from
   `PostToolUse` for an undelivered request (resolves `terminal`, replies
   empty to release the hold), or from the captured dialog leaving the
   screen. Changes it: if allow also closed the connection, nothing in
   the plan breaks; the `PostToolUse` path just fires less often.
3. **PENDING GATE 2: held hook while another hook decides; late
   replies (2c, 2d).** Draft: PASS. Claude does not wait for the held hook, ignores
   a late reply, and the evidence is as §6.2 says (deny leaves
   `UserPromptSubmit`/`Stop` without `PostToolUse`). Plan: Task 11b as
   written. Changes it: if a late reply were applied, P3 would have to
   stop replying to holds after another decider, which it cannot know;
   that would be a blocker.
4. **PENDING GATE 2: `allow_always` persistence (2e).** Draft: persists
   with `addRules`/`localSettings` in a new session, but Bash requests
   carry **no** `addRules` suggestion, so under spec §6.2 the option is
   never offered for Bash. Plan: follows the spec (offer only when a
   suggestion has `addRules`; destination forced to `localSettings`).
   See open question Q2 (construct the rule from the command).
5. **PENDING GATE 2: stalled endpoint (2f).** Draft: PASS; lifecycle
   hooks proceed after 1.0 s. Plan: lifecycle timeout 1 s (Task 16a).
6. **PENDING GATE 2: SessionStart is not an http hook (extra finding).** Draft:
   Claude never sends an http request for `SessionStart` (the docs allow
   only `command` and `mcp_tool` there). Plan: Claude entries omit
   `SessionStart`; hooks-only Claude agents come from the registry scan
   and from any later hook (Tasks 8, 10, 16). Changes it: nothing to add
   if SessionStart stays unsupported.
7. **PENDING GATE 4: Codex binding.** Draft: `HERDR_*` in Codex hooks is
   the env of the app-server process and is stale or wrong in the default
   shared-daemon mode (reproduced on 0.162.0, not cured by a fresh
   server). herdr's `agent_session` for Codex is `{source, agent, kind:
   "id", value}` and equals the hook `session_id`, but is **absent** in
   shared-daemon mode and can be attached to the wrong pane. Two Codex
   panes in one cwd cannot be told apart. Plan (Tasks 9 and 13): the env
   is never used for Codex, not even for ordering. Bind by (1) a codex
   pane whose `agent_session.value == session_id`, or (2) the **only**
   codex pane in the canonicalized cwd. Either way a keys target is
   registered only after that pane's own detection snapshot shows the
   dialog with the request's command. Two or more codex panes in the cwd
   and no session match: no request; the herdr fallback shows the dialog
   read-only. Changes it: if the orchestrator prefers the spec's looser
   rule (several panes in the cwd, exactly one showing the dialog), Task
   9's `codex_bind` gains that branch (see open question Q3).
8. **PENDING GATE 5: Claude session registry.** Draft: fields as in
   `tests/fixtures/hooks/claude/session-registry-entry.json`; `status`
   is `idle`, `busy` or `waiting` (with `waitingFor`); `procStart` is
   `ps -o lstart=` in UTC (`Fri Oct  9 07:29:36 2026`); a `kill -9`
   leaves the entry behind; truncated, empty and mode-0 files fail to
   parse or open. Plan (Task 8): `idle` -> `idle` (a hook-set `done`
   stays `done`), `busy` -> `working`, `waiting` -> `needs_you`, anything
   else -> `unknown`. An entry is live only when the pid exists and its
   OS start time equals the parsed `procStart` within 1 s. Changes it: a
   different `procStart` format changes only `parse_lstart_utc`.
9. **PENDING GATE 7: hook token rotation.** Draft: running Claude
   sessions keep the token they started with for their whole life (a
   literal header is re-read only on `/clear`, resume or restart). A
   rejected token is non-blocking but prints `hook error / HTTP 401` on
   every lifecycle hook. The spec's 10-minute grace is too short. Plan
   (Task 17): after `acd token rotate hook`, the previous token stays
   valid until no live registry session that started before the rotation
   remains (sessions seen using the new token are excluded), with a
   30-day cap; a failed rewrite keeps it valid until a later
   `acd hooks install claude` succeeds and then applies the same rule.
   `acd status` shows the pending count. Changes it: if the orchestrator
   keeps the spec's 10 minutes, Task 17's `retire_previous` becomes a
   timestamp check (see open question Q4).
10. **LIVE CHECK (not a numbered gate): Claude tip lines.** P2 keeps
    `Tip:` lines in the dialog identity. If a tip rotates while the same
    dialog stays up, the target watch would publish a false `terminal`.
    Task 20 Step 6 checks it live before keys requests are enabled for
    users, and records the result.
11. **LIVE CHECK: Codex user-level hook trust.** Gate 4 saw hook-trust
    prompts for project hooks, which write `[hooks.state."…"]` into
    `~/.codex/config.toml`. Whether `~/.codex/hooks.json` written by
    `acd hooks install codex` also needs a one-time trust review is
    unverified. Task 16b prints a note; Task 20 Step 6 records the result.

## Orchestrator rulings (2026-10-09)

- **Q1:** accepted. The gate results are committed (a6e08dd) and are the
  basis. Task 20 folds them into the spec.
- **Q2:** adopt in P3. The rule is the exact command
  (`{toolName: "Bash", ruleContent: <command>}`, `localSettings`), and the
  client shows that exact rule before sending (spec §6.2). Note that paths
  outside the cwd may still prompt.
- **Q3:** keep the stricter rule. A wrong bind sends keys to the wrong
  agent; a read-only pane only costs convenience.
- **Q4:** accepted, with the 30-day cap.
- **Q5:** accept both D13 and D14. Held hooks fail open, so a restart is
  safe.
- **Q6:** accept D10 (`uncertain`). No new core state in P3.
- **Q7:** acceptable for v1. The install note explains embedded mode.
- **Q8:** yes. Sol reviews the plan before execution.

## Open questions for the orchestrator

- **Q1. Gate drafts.** Accept the draft gate results as the basis for
  this plan (pending items 1 to 9), and let Task 20 fold them into the
  spec once confirmed?
- **Q2. `allow_always` for Bash.** Under spec §6.2 the option needs an
  `addRules` suggestion, which Bash requests never carry (P0 item 2,
  gate 2e), so "Yes, always" is never offered for Bash. P0 and gate 2e
  both recommend building the rule from the command
  (`{toolName: "Bash", ruleContent: <command>}`, `localSettings`) and
  warning that paths outside the working directory may still prompt.
  The change is contained in `wire::always_rule` and
  `mapping::claude_permission`. Adopt it in P3 or defer?
- **Q3. Codex cwd rule.** The plan binds by cwd only when exactly one
  Codex pane is in that cwd (gate 4 draft). The spec allows binding when
  several panes share the cwd but exactly one shows the dialog. The
  stricter rule avoids a race where two panes show the same command
  within the 35 to 118 ms render delay. Keep the stricter rule?
- **Q4. Hook token retention.** The plan keeps the previous token until
  every Claude session that started before the rotation has exited (30
  days at most), per the gate 7 draft, instead of the spec's 10 minutes.
  Accept?
- **Q5. Daemon failure policy.** D13 (hook port bind failure is fatal)
  and D14 (any panic exits with 70 and the supervisor restarts acd,
  dropping held hooks, which fail open). Accept both?
- **Q6. Supersede while awaiting evidence (D10).** A delivered answer
  whose next prompt arrives before `PostToolUse` resolves `uncertain`.
  Accept, or add a "pending evidence" state to the core now?
- **Q7. Codex in the default shared-daemon mode.** With one Codex pane
  per cwd, approvals are answerable (§14 item 2 holds). With two Codex
  panes in one cwd and no `agent_session`, they stay read-only. Is that
  acceptable for v1, with the install note pointing users at embedded
  mode?
- **Q8. Plan review.** This plan changes auth (hook tokens, rotation),
  writes into user settings files, and installs a launchd agent. Per the
  routing policy it should get a Sol review before execution.

## Global Constraints

These apply to every task.

- **Workspace rules.** Rust edition 2024, `rust-version = "1.90"`. CI
  runs `cargo test --workspace --locked` and
  `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` on
  macOS and Linux, plus `npm run verify` in `app/`. Every task ends with
  both cargo commands green; tasks that touch protocol types also run
  `cd app && npm run verify`.
- **Never touch the developer's real environment in tests.** No test
  may read or write the real `~/.claude`, `~/.codex`,
  `~/Library/LaunchAgents`, `~/.config/systemd`, `~/.config/acd`, the
  real herdr socket, or the desktop T3. Every test that runs the `acd`
  binary sets `HOME` and `ACD_CONFIG_DIR` to a temp dir and removes
  `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`,
  `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` and `RUST_LOG`. Library tests pass paths
  explicitly and never call `std::env::var("HOME")`. No test calls
  `launchctl` or `systemctl`; the service installer takes an injectable
  runner. Git, when a test needs it, runs with `HOME` set to the temp
  dir, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`.
- **Ports in tests.** No test binds a fixed port. Daemon tests pick
  free ports for both `port` and `hook_port` and retry the whole start
  when the daemon reports a bind failure (Task 1).
- **Hook endpoint** (spec §6.1, §7.1):
  - Binds `127.0.0.1:<hook_port>` only (default 47801). Never `0.0.0.0`,
    never a Tailscale address.
  - `POST /hook/{agent}/{event}` with `agent` in `claude|codex`. Every
    other method, path or agent gets 401. The query string is ignored
    (it carries the install marker).
  - `X-Acd-Token` is compared in constant time against the current
    token and, while retained, the previous one. An empty stored token
    never authenticates. Missing or wrong token: 401.
  - Body at most 1 MiB (413 above), read within 5 s. A body that is not
    a JSON object gets `200` with an empty body (fail open).
  - Header hints (`X-Herdr-Pane`, `X-Herdr-Socket`,
    `X-Claude-Entrypoint`) are accepted only when non-empty, at most 1024
    bytes, and free of control characters; otherwise they are absent.
  - `X-Herdr-Socket` is a lookup key only. acd never opens, stats or
    connects to a path taken from a header. It maps the value through
    `herdr::map::socket_hash` to an already-discovered socket.
  - Ids from payloads (`session_id`, `prompt_id`, `turn_id`, pane ids)
    are accepted only when 1 to 128 bytes with no control characters.
- **Fail open** (invariant 1). Lifecycle hooks get their reply before any
  processing. Only Claude `PermissionRequest` for a tool other than
  `AskUserQuestion` is ever held, and only for ownership rules 2 and 3
  (herdr-bound or hooks-owned). Every other hook, every T3-owned,
  unknown-ownership or subagent session, and every hold beyond 64
  concurrent holds gets an empty reply at once. A hold is released with
  an empty reply as soon as acd knows its request ended, was superseded,
  migrated away, or dropped for T3 ownership.
- **One ordered consumer.** All hook events go through one unbounded
  mailbox drained by the hooks reconciler task, in arrival order. No
  per-request tasks touch hook state. The reconciler never awaits a herdr
  call; reads run in spawned tasks and post results back.
- **Exclusive answer paths** (spec §5.1). A held request has
  `answer_path: hook`. It switches to `keys` only after its hold has
  ended (closed or expired), and only by an observation sent while the
  per-agent delivery lock (Task 4) is held. No keys target is registered
  for a request while its hold is open.
- **Keys targets** (P1 follow-ups). Built only with
  `KeysTarget::from_screen`, from a detection snapshot read for this
  request. Registered only after `core.agent(agent_id)` is `Some` and
  the request is still `open`, with `request_id =
  core.request_id_for(agent_id, &key)`. Option keys are `"1"`..`"9"` or
  `"esc"`, never Enter. A target is immutable for its request id (Task
  4). Approval decisions never use `Dialog::text` or
  `Dialog::identity`; those only match a hook to a dialog and test
  presence.
- **Correlation keys** (spec §5.1 as amended by Decision D2):
  - Claude: `claude:<session_id>:<prompt_id>:<tool digest>`, reusable.
  - Codex: `codex:<session_id>:<turn_id>:<tool digest>`, reusable.
  - Tool digest: lowercase hex `sha256(tool_name ‖ 0x00 ‖
    canonical_json(tool_input))`, keys sorted, no whitespace.
  - Keys are scoped to their agent; a migrated request takes its keys
    with it (Task 2).
- **Attribution** (spec §5.1, §6.2). `inbox` only when acd delivered and
  the correlated outcome is consistent; `terminal` when it contradicts or
  the dialog ended without acd delivery; `uncertain` when acd delivered
  and the evidence is missing within 30 s. Evidence used for a request is
  only evidence from the same session that arrived after the delivery
  time. A verdict is never reused across request ids.
- **Display text.** Every string from a hook payload or the session
  registry that reaches a client goes through `text::clean_display`
  (titles, labels, cap 200 chars) or `text::clean_screen` (detail, cap
  4000 chars). `last_message` is capped at 500 chars.
- **Logs.** Never log a token, a header value, or the raw body above
  `debug`. Payloads are logged only at `debug`, through
  `text::redact_log` (4 KiB). Rejected tokens are logged at `warn` at
  most once per minute per remote hint, without the token.
- **Files holding the hook token** (spec §6.1). `~/.claude/settings.json`
  (or `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/settings.json`) only, never project settings.
  Refuse a symlinked settings file. Write it atomically with mode 0600.
  Back up to `<config_dir>/backups/` (dir 0700, files 0600). Refuse a
  Git-tracked settings file unless `--allow-tracked`. `hook.token`,
  `hook.token.prev` and `hook-rotation.json` are 0600 in the 0700
  config dir; preflight checks them.
- **Locks.** No `std::sync` lock is held across an `.await` or a `Core`
  call. Clone out first.

## Review Focus

These are the inputs most likely to hurt a real user that no task's
happy path covers. Each has a pinned test in the task named.

1. **A held Claude prompt answered "Yes" in the terminal.** The hold
   stays open (gate 2b). acd must see `PostToolUse` for the undelivered
   request, resolve it `terminal`, reply empty, and never later deliver
   an inbox answer to that hold. Test: Task 11b
   (`terminal_allow_resolves_terminal_and_releases_the_hold`).
2. **Two permission prompts in one Claude turn.** They share `prompt_id`.
   The second must open a new request after the first resolves, and a
   held first request must not be overwritten. Tests: Task 6
   (`same_turn_different_tools_get_different_keys`), Task 11b
   (`a_second_prompt_in_the_same_turn_opens_a_new_request`,
   `the_same_command_after_an_uncertain_end_opens_again`).
3. **A Codex hook whose env names another live pane.** Pane ids repeat
   across herdr sessions and the shared daemon's env is stale (gate 4).
   No key may ever reach a pane that is not showing this request's
   command. Tests: Task 9 (`codex_env_is_never_used_for_binding`), Task
   13 (`stale_env_never_binds_and_two_panes_in_one_cwd_stay_read_only`).
4. **Installer against a real-world settings file.** Foreign hooks in
   the same event, Claude's own reformatting between install and
   uninstall, a symlinked or Git-tracked file, and running install twice.
   The result after uninstall is semantically identical to the original
   and nothing foreign moves. Tests: Task 16b
   (`install_twice_then_uninstall_is_identity`,
   `foreign_entries_keep_their_order_and_bytes`,
   `refuses_symlink_and_tracked_settings`,
   `uninstall_survives_claude_reformatting`).
5. **Hook token in logs and on the wire.** At `trace`, the daemon log
   never contains the hook token after a real hook POST; the shim never
   prints it; the installer prints only the path. Tests: Task 19
   (`trace_logs_never_contain_either_token`), Task 15
   (`the_shim_forwards_the_hook_and_prints_the_reply`), Task 16b
   (`claude_install_writes_owned_entries_with_the_token_at_mode_0600`),
   Task 17 (`hook_rotation_rewrites_managed_headers_and_keeps_the_previous_token`).

## Decisions

- **D1. One adapter for both agents.** `HooksAdapter` owns
  `AnswerPath::Hook` and `Source::Hooks`. Claude and Codex share the
  endpoint, the reconciler, ownership and evidence. Codex never uses the
  hook answer path (it only replies empty) and has no hooks-only agents.
- **D2. Claude keys include the tool digest and are reusable.** The
  fixtures show `prompt_id` is per user turn
  (`payload-UserPromptSubmit`, `payload-PermissionRequest-Bash`,
  `payload-Notification` and `payload-Stop` share `e7cf77ea…`). A bare
  `prompt_id` key would merge two prompts of one turn (registry step 1)
  or drop the second as a tombstoned replay (step 2). The key
  `claude:<session>:<prompt_id>:<digest>` still links the
  `AskUserQuestion` `PreToolUse` and `PermissionRequest` pair, which
  carry the same input. It is reusable: acd's own end of the request
  confirms the occurrence gone (`RequestEndedById` with a confirming
  `by`), so a repeat of the same command in the same turn opens a new
  request. This amends spec §5.1's table.
- **D3. Codex evidence matches the command, not the key digest.** Codex
  `PostToolUse.tool_input` drops `description`
  (`codex/payload-PostToolUse-Bash.json`). Evidence for Codex compares
  `session_id`, `tool_name` and `tool_input.command`.
- **D4. No hooks-only Codex agents.** Spec §5 has no agent id for a
  Codex session outside herdr and T3. An unbound Codex request opens
  nothing; the herdr fallback (`blocked_unparsed`) shows it read-only
  when the pane is in herdr.
- **D5. Codex cwd comparison.** herdr reports `cwd: /tmp` and
  `foreground_cwd: /private/tmp/…` for a Codex pane
  (`api/agent.get.codex-idle.json`). P3 decodes `foreground_cwd`, prefers
  it, and compares canonicalized paths (`std::fs::canonicalize`, falling
  back to the string without a trailing `/`).
- **D6. Migration is a core operation.** `Observation::AgentMigrated {
  from, to }` moves the pending request (same id, same status, same
  revision), its keys and generation counters, from the hooks-only agent
  to the herdr agent, then removes `from`. A `blocked_unparsed` fallback
  pending on `to` resolves `superseded`. A structured request pending on
  `to` wins; the migrating one resolves `lost` (or `uncertain` if it was
  being answered).
- **D7. Per-agent delivery lock.** P2's keys engine takes an async lock
  per agent from just before its lease check until its key write
  completes. P3 takes the same lock around every observation that opens a
  structured request on a herdr agent, switches a path to `keys`, or
  migrates into a herdr agent. This closes the cross-path window the P2
  review flagged. The lock does not span herdr's reply wait, so it is
  held for at most the local write.
- **D8. Keys targets are set-once.** `KeysTargets::set` accepts a target
  for a request id only once (an identical re-set is a no-op). A
  different target for the same id is refused. This binds the target to
  the content the user reviewed: a request whose dialog changes gets a
  new request id from the registry, never a replaced target (answer
  revision obligation).
- **D9. Claude permission keys after the hold.** When a held request
  switches to `keys`, its options are rebuilt: `allow` "Yes" (key `1`)
  and `deny` "No" (the number of the dialog option labelled `No`, or
  `esc` when none). `allow_always` is dropped, because Claude's dialog
  option 2 adds a directory rule, not the `addRules` rule the client
  showed. The rebuild is a semantic change and bumps the revision.
- **D10. Supersede while awaiting evidence.** If the next permission
  request of the same agent arrives while an earlier one is `answering`
  (delivered, waiting for `PostToolUse`), the registry resolves the
  earlier one `uncertain` (registry rule 4). Claude asks permissions one
  at a time, so the next prompt shows only that Claude processed some
  decision, not which one. The adapter keeps watching the old request's
  evidence for 30 s and logs at `debug` whether it later matched, so the
  live e2e can measure how often this costs attribution.
- **D11. Registry status vs hook state.** Hook events are fresher than
  the 30 s scan. A scan never turns a hook-set `done` into `idle`, and a
  scan state is applied only when no hook event for the session arrived
  in the last 30 s.
- **D12. Hook token rotation keeps one previous token.** A second
  rotation while the first is pending drops the oldest token and reports
  how many live sessions still used it.
- **D13. Hook listener bind failure is fatal**, like the acd/1 listeners.
  A daemon that cannot serve hooks would silently drop every permission
  prompt to the terminal.
- **D14. Panic exits the daemon.** `acd run` installs a panic hook that
  logs and exits with code 70. This covers the "exit when a mutex is
  poisoned" obligation (a poison needs a panic first), and the supervisor
  restarts acd. Library tests are unaffected.
- **D15. Supervision.** SIGTERM and SIGINT shut down gracefully with exit
  0. A dead server task exits with code 1. Tailscale address changes keep
  exit 75. launchd uses `KeepAlive.SuccessfulExit = false`; systemd uses
  `Restart=on-failure`. Both restart on 1, 70 and 75 and not on 0.
- **D16. Settings edits preserve order without changing serde_json
  globally.** The installer parses the top-level object and the `hooks`
  object as ordered lists of `(key, RawValue)`, so foreign keys, events
  and entries keep their order and bytes. Only owned groups are parsed
  and rewritten. serde_json's `preserve_order` is not enabled, because it
  would change map ordering for every crate in the workspace.
- **D17. The shim forwards `HERDR_PANE_ID` and `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH` as
  headers** (spec §6.1 says it adds `HERDR_*` hints). P3 logs them at
  debug for Codex and never binds with them (gate 4). No `CODEX_*`
  variable is forwarded; none is used.
- **D18. `last_message` only for hooks-only agents.** herdr owns the
  state of herdr agents. Writing `last_message` from `Stop` onto a herdr
  agent would race the herdr reconciler's upserts.
- **D19. herdr `state_change_seq` stays log-only** (P1 follow-up). The
  periodic list applies every pane anyway. No code change.
- **D20. `find_dialog` stays fail-closed on a status line below the
  footer** (P1 follow-up). No code change; Task 20's live check records
  whether the current Claude and Codex versions draw one.
- **D21. T3 decode-error degradation already clears** after 10 minutes
  without new errors (`t3/reconciler.rs`, `last_decode_error`). The P1
  follow-up is satisfied by P4; no change.

Two chains share no files: the core/herdr/hooks chain (2, 3, 4, 5, then
9, 10, 11a to 11c, 12, 13) and the `acd` binary chain (1, 14, 15, 16a,
16b, 17, 18). They meet in
Task 19. Tasks 6, 7, 8 and 11a only add new files, except that each adds one
`pub mod` line to `crates/acd-adapters/src/hooks/mod.rs`; that one-line
merge conflict is expected and the integration owner resolves it.
Task 6 adds every new `acd-adapters` dependency, so later tasks never
edit that `Cargo.toml`. Every `crates/acd/src/main.rs`
edit is in the binary chain, which runs one task at a time. Integration
owner: the orchestrator merges task branches into `p3-hooks` in table
order and runs the full workspace checks after each merge. A task that
finds it must edit a file owned by a task running in parallel stops and
reports.

### Task 1: Test harness: free ports with retry, environment isolation

Fixes the unlogged CI flake (macOS, 2026-10-09): `free_port()` in
`crates/acd/tests/cli.rs` binds port 0, drops the listener and returns
the port, and a parallel test can take that port before the daemon binds
it. The daemon then exits with `cannot bind`, and `status_when_ready`
fails after 10 s. P3 adds a second listener (`hook_port`), which doubles
the exposure, so the fix covers both ports and lives in a shared module.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/acd/tests/common/mod.rs`
- Modify: `crates/acd/tests/cli.rs` (use `common`; delete its `acd`,
  `free_port`, `Daemon`, `init_loopback`)
- Modify: `crates/acd/tests/daemon_tls.rs` (use `common`; delete its
  `acd`, `Daemon`, `init`, `start`)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (later `crates/acd/tests/*.rs` files use these exact names):
  - `common::acd(dir: &Path) -> Command`: sets `ACD_CONFIG_DIR=dir`,
    `HOME=dir`, removes `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`,
    `CODEX_HOME`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`, `RUST_LOG`.
  - `common::acd_home(dir: &Path, home: &Path) -> Command`: the same,
    with a separate `HOME`.
  - `common::free_port() -> u16`.
  - `common::Daemon(pub Child)`: killed and reaped on drop.
  - `common::Started { pub daemon: Daemon, pub port: u16, pub hook_port: u16, pub log: PathBuf }`.
  - `common::start_daemon(dir: &Path, extra_config: &str, env: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Started`.
  - `common::start_daemon_with(dir, extra_config, env, pick: impl FnMut() -> (u16, u16)) -> Started`.
  - `common::init_dir(parent: &Path) -> PathBuf`: runs `acd init` in
    `parent/acd` and returns it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `crates/acd/tests/common/mod.rs` with only the test first, at the
bottom of a new `crates/acd/tests/harness.rs`:

```rust
mod common;

#[test]
fn start_retries_when_a_port_is_taken_before_the_daemon_binds() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let dir = common::init_dir(tmp.path());
    let blocker = std::net::TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").unwrap();
    let taken = blocker.local_addr().unwrap().port();
    let mut picks = vec![(taken, common::free_port()), (common::free_port(), common::free_port())].into_iter();
    let started = common::start_daemon_with(&dir, "", &[], || picks.next().expect("at most two attempts"));
    assert_ne!(started.port, taken, "the second attempt must be used");
    assert!(dir.join("acd.sock").exists());
}

#[test]
fn every_command_is_isolated_from_the_user_environment() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let cmd = common::acd(tmp.path());
    let envs: Vec<_> = cmd.get_envs().map(|(k, v)| (k.to_owned(), v.map(|v| v.to_owned()))).collect();
    for k in ["HERDR_SOCKET_PATH", "CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR", "CODEX_HOME", "CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT", "RUST_LOG"] {
        assert!(envs.iter().any(|(n, v)| n == k && v.is_none()), "{k} must be removed");
    }
    assert!(envs.iter().any(|(n, v)| n == "HOME" && v.as_deref() == Some(tmp.path().as_os_str())));
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the shared module**

`crates/acd/tests/common/mod.rs`:

```rust
//! Shared helpers for tests that run the `acd` binary. Every command is
//! isolated from the developer's environment: HOME and ACD_CONFIG_DIR
//! point into a temp dir, and variables that would reach real herdr,
//! Claude or Codex state are removed.
#![allow(dead_code)]
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::process::{Child, Command, Stdio};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

const ISOLATED: [&str; 5] = [
    "HERDR_SOCKET_PATH",
    "CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR",
    "CODEX_HOME",
    "CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT",
    "RUST_LOG",
];

pub fn acd(dir: &Path) -> Command {
    acd_home(dir, dir)
}

pub fn acd_home(dir: &Path, home: &Path) -> Command {
    let mut c = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_acd"));
    c.env("ACD_CONFIG_DIR", dir).env("HOME", home);
    for k in ISOLATED {
        c.env_remove(k);
    }
    c
}

pub fn free_port() -> u16 {
    std::net::TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0")
        .unwrap()
        .local_addr()
        .unwrap()
        .port()
}

/// A running `acd run`, killed when dropped so a failed assert cannot
/// leave it behind.
pub struct Daemon(pub Child);

impl Drop for Daemon {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.0.kill().ok();
        self.0.wait().ok();
    }
}

pub struct Started {
    pub daemon: Daemon,
    pub port: u16,
    pub hook_port: u16,
    pub log: PathBuf,
}

pub fn init_dir(parent: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    let dir = parent.join("acd");
    let out = acd(&dir).arg("init").output().unwrap();
    assert!(out.status.success(), "{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr));
    dir
}

/// Writes `config.toml` with fresh ports and starts `acd run`, retrying
/// the whole start when the daemon could not bind (another test took the
/// port between `free_port` and the bind).
pub fn start_daemon(dir: &Path, extra_config: &str, env: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Started {
    start_daemon_with(dir, extra_config, env, || (free_port(), free_port()))
}

pub fn start_daemon_with(
    dir: &Path,
    extra_config: &str,
    env: &[(&str, &str)],
    mut pick: impl FnMut() -> (u16, u16),
) -> Started {
    for attempt in 0..5 {
        let (port, hook_port) = pick();
        // A caller that sets `listen` itself gets no default (TOML forbids
        // duplicate keys).
        let listen = if extra_config.contains("listen =") { "" } else { "listen = [\"loopback\"]\n" };
        // Overwriting keeps the 0600 mode `acd init` gave the file.
        std::fs::write(
            dir.join("config.toml"),
            format!("port = {port}\nhook_port = {hook_port}\n{listen}{extra_config}"),
        )
        .unwrap();
        let log = dir.join(format!("daemon-{attempt}.log"));
        let mut cmd = acd(dir);
        for (k, v) in env {
            cmd.env(k, v);
        }
        let child = cmd
            .arg("run")
            .stdout(Stdio::null())
            .stderr(std::fs::File::create(&log).unwrap())
            .spawn()
            .unwrap();
        let mut daemon = Daemon(child);
        let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(10);
        loop {
            if dir.join("acd.sock").exists() {
                return Started { daemon, port, hook_port, log };
            }
            if let Some(status) = daemon.0.try_wait().unwrap() {
                let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&log).unwrap_or_default();
                if text.contains("cannot bind") || text.contains("Address already in use") {
                    break; // retry with new ports
                }
                panic!("acd run exited with {status}: {text}");
            }
            assert!(Instant::now() < deadline, "daemon did not start");
            std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20));
        }
    }
    panic!("could not start acd after 5 attempts");
}
```

`hook_port` is ignored by `Config` until Task 19 (unknown keys are
ignored), so the helper is correct before and after that task.

- [ ] **Step 4: Move `cli.rs` and `daemon_tls.rs` onto the module**

In `crates/acd/tests/cli.rs`:

- Add `mod common;` and `use common::{acd, free_port, Daemon};` at the
  top. Delete the local `acd`, `free_port` and `Daemon`.
- Replace `init_loopback(name)` with a version that only initializes and
  sets the machine name, and start daemons through `start_daemon`:

```rust
/// An initialized config dir; `start` writes ports and starts the daemon.
fn init_named(name: &str) -> (tempfile::TempDir, std::path::PathBuf) {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let dir = common::init_dir(tmp.path());
    (tmp, dir)
}

fn start(dir: &std::path::Path, name: &str, env: &[(&str, &str)]) -> common::Started {
    common::start_daemon(dir, &format!("machine_name = \"{name}\"\n"), env)
}
```

- `run_serves_loopback_and_status_reports_machine`: replace
  `init_loopback("e2e-box")` and `Daemon(acd(&dir).arg("run").spawn())`
  with `init_named` and `let _d = start(&dir, "e2e-box", &[]);`. Keep
  the stale-socket line before `start`.
- `trace_logs_never_contain_the_token`: use
  `let started = start(&dir, "log-box", &[("RUST_LOG", rust_log)]);` and
  read `started.log` instead of the hand-made log file. Drop the daemon
  (`drop(started)`) before reading the log.
- `second_daemon_on_another_port_leaves_the_live_socket_alone`: start
  the first with `start`; the second keeps `run_expecting_exit` with a
  config written by hand (`port = {free_port()}`, `hook_port =
  {free_port()}`). A second daemon that loses the port race also exits,
  and the assertion accepts either message:

```rust
assert!(err.contains("another acd is running") || err.contains("cannot bind"), "{err}");
```

  then re-run `run_expecting_exit` once if the message was `cannot
  bind`, and require `another acd is running` on that run.
- Tests that call `run_expecting_exit` and expect an early failure
  (`run_refuses_empty_or_short_client_token`,
  `run_fails_when_a_listen_address_cannot_be_bound`) keep their
  hand-written configs; they never reach a bind or bind a port held by
  the test itself.

In `crates/acd/tests/daemon_tls.rs`: add `mod common;`, delete `acd`,
`Daemon`, `init` and `start`, and replace them with:

```rust
/// Callers that passed `listen` to the old `init` now pass it to `start`.
fn init() -> (tempfile::TempDir, std::path::PathBuf) {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let dir = common::init_dir(tmp.path());
    (tmp, dir)
}

fn start(dir: &std::path::Path, listen: &str, debug_log: bool, admission: Option<usize>) -> common::Started {
    let mut env: Vec<(&str, String)> = Vec::new();
    if debug_log { env.push(("RUST_LOG", "debug".into())); }
    if let Some(n) = admission { env.push(("ACD_TEST_MAX_UNAUTH", n.to_string())); }
    let env: Vec<(&str, &str)> = env.iter().map(|(k, v)| (*k, v.as_str())).collect();
    common::start_daemon(dir, &format!("listen = {listen}\n"), &env)
}
```

The helper omits its default `listen` line because `extra_config` sets
one. Callers read `started.port` where they used the returned port, and
`started.log` where they passed a log path.

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `Observation::AgentMigrated { from: String, to: String }`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`crates/acd-core/tests/migrate.rs`:

```rust
use acd_core::testing::{agent_obs, permission_obs};
use acd_core::*;
use acd_protocol::*;

fn hooks_agent(id: &str) -> AgentObs {
    AgentObs { source: Source::Hooks, can_send: false, ..agent_obs(id) }
}

fn reg() -> Registry {
    let mut r = Registry::new("m".into());
    r.apply(Observation::AgentUpsert(hooks_agent("claude:s1")));
    r.apply(Observation::AgentUpsert(agent_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1")));
    r
}

#[test]
fn the_open_request_moves_and_keeps_its_id() {
    let mut r = reg();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    let rid = r.agent("claude:s1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    let changes = r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert!(matches!(&changes[0], Change::AgentUpdated(a) if a.id == "herdr:h:w1:p1"
        && a.pending.as_ref().map(|p| p.id.as_str()) == Some(rid.as_str())
        && a.state == AgentState::NeedsYou));
    assert_eq!(changes.last(), Some(&Change::AgentRemoved("claude:s1".into())));
    assert!(!changes.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { .. } | Change::RequestOpened(_))));
    assert!(r.agent("claude:s1").is_none());
    assert_eq!(r.request(&rid).unwrap().agent_id, "herdr:h:w1:p1");
    // The key moved too: a replay on the new agent updates in place.
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1", "k")));
    assert_eq!(r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id, rid);
    let ended = r.apply(Observation::RequestEnded {
        agent_id: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into(), key: CorrelationKey("k".into()), by: ResolvedBy::Terminal,
    });
    assert!(ended.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { request_id, .. } if *request_id == rid)));
}

#[test]
fn a_claimed_request_moves_with_its_claim() {
    let mut r = reg();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    let rid = r.agent("claude:s1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    r.claim(&rid, Answer::Option("allow".into()), None).unwrap();
    r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert_eq!(r.request(&rid).unwrap().status, RequestStatus::Answering);
    let done = r.apply(Observation::RequestEndedById { request_id: rid.clone(), by: ResolvedBy::Inbox });
    assert!(done.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { option_id: Some(o), .. } if o == "allow")));
}

#[test]
fn a_fallback_on_the_target_is_superseded() {
    let mut r = reg();
    let mut fb = permission_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1", "herdr:h:w1:p1:fp");
    fb.kind = RequestKind::BlockedUnparsed;
    fb.answer_path = AnswerPath::None;
    r.apply(Observation::FallbackSeen(fb));
    let fb_id = r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    let changes = r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert!(changes.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { request_id, by: ResolvedBy::Superseded, .. } if *request_id == fb_id)));
    assert_ne!(r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id, fb_id);
}

#[test]
fn a_structured_request_on_the_target_wins() {
    let mut r = reg();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1", "other")));
    let kept = r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    let moving = r.agent("claude:s1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    let changes = r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert!(changes.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { request_id, by: ResolvedBy::Lost, .. } if *request_id == moving)));
    assert_eq!(r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id, kept);
    assert!(r.agent("claude:s1").is_none());
}

#[test]
fn migration_without_a_request_only_removes_the_source() {
    let mut r = reg();
    let changes = r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert_eq!(changes, vec![Change::AgentRemoved("claude:s1".into())]);
}

#[test]
fn migration_to_an_unknown_agent_is_a_no_op() {
    let mut r = reg();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    assert!(r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:nope".into() }).is_empty());
    assert!(r.agent("claude:s1").unwrap().pending.is_some());
}

#[test]
fn pending_key_names_the_primary_key() {
    let mut r = reg();
    assert!(r.pending_key("herdr:h:w1:p1").is_none());
    let mut fb = permission_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1", "herdr:h:w1:p1:fp");
    fb.kind = RequestKind::BlockedUnparsed;
    fb.answer_path = AnswerPath::None;
    r.apply(Observation::FallbackSeen(fb));
    let (rid, key, kind) = r.pending_key("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(key, CorrelationKey("herdr:h:w1:p1:fp".into()));
    assert_eq!(kind, RequestKind::BlockedUnparsed);
    assert_eq!(r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id, rid);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

In `registry.rs`, add the variant to `Observation`:

```rust
    /// Moves `from`'s pending request (same id, status and revision) and
    /// its keys to `to`, then removes `from` (spec §6.2 Binding, D6).
    AgentMigrated {
        from: String,
        to: String,
    },
```

and the arm in `apply`:

```rust
            Observation::AgentMigrated { from, to } => self.migrate(&from, &to),
```

Add to `impl Registry`:

```rust
    pub fn pending_key(&self, agent_id: &str) -> Option<(String, CorrelationKey, RequestKind)> {
        let p = self.agents.get(agent_id)?.pending.as_ref()?;
        let e = self.requests.get(&p.id)?;
        Some((p.id.clone(), e.key.clone(), e.req.kind))
    }

    fn migrate(&mut self, from: &str, to: &str) -> Vec<Change> {
        if from == to || !self.agents.contains_key(from) || !self.agents.contains_key(to) {
            return vec![];
        }
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let moving = self.agents.get(from).and_then(|a| a.pending.clone());
        if let Some(p) = moving {
            match self.agents.get(to).and_then(|a| a.pending.clone()) {
                Some(q) if q.kind == RequestKind::BlockedUnparsed && q.status == RequestStatus::Open => {
                    out.extend(
                        self.resolve_request(&q.id, ResolvedBy::Superseded)
                            .into_iter()
                            .filter(|c| !matches!(c, Change::AgentUpdated(_))),
                    );
                }
                Some(_) => {
                    let by = if p.status == RequestStatus::Answering {
                        ResolvedBy::Uncertain
                    } else {
                        ResolvedBy::Lost
                    };
                    out.extend(
                        self.resolve_request(&p.id, by)
                            .into_iter()
                            .filter(|c| !matches!(c, Change::AgentUpdated(_))),
                    );
                    out.extend(self.agent_gone(from));
                    return out;
                }
                None => {}
            }
            if let Some(e) = self.requests.get_mut(&p.id) {
                e.req.agent_id = to.to_string();
            }
            let moved: Vec<(String, CorrelationKey)> = self
                .keys
                .keys()
                .filter(|(a, _)| a == from)
                .cloned()
                .collect();
            for (a, k) in moved {
                let mut st = self.keys.remove(&(a, k.clone())).expect("listed above");
                st.agent_id = to.to_string();
                self.keys.entry((to.to_string(), k)).or_insert(st);
            }
            let req = self.requests.get(&p.id).map(|e| e.req.clone()).expect("moved request");
            self.seen.remove(to);
            let a = self.agents.get_mut(to).expect("checked above");
            a.pending = Some(req);
            if a.state != AgentState::NeedsYou {
                a.state = AgentState::NeedsYou;
                a.state_since = Utc::now();
            }
            out.push(Change::AgentUpdated(a.clone()));
        }
        // The request moved: nothing is resolved here.
        self.agents.remove(from);
        self.keys.retain(|_, k| k.agent_id != from);
        self.seen.remove(from);
        out.push(Change::AgentRemoved(from.to_string()));
        out
    }
```

In `engine.rs`, next to `request_id_for`:

```rust
    pub fn pending_key(&self, agent_id: &str) -> Option<(String, CorrelationKey, RequestKind)> {
        self.inner.lock().unwrap().registry.pending_key(agent_id)
    }
```

(`RequestKind` comes from `acd_protocol::*`, already imported.)

### Task 3: Resolution `reason` on `request.resolved`

P2's keys engine already resolves a stray keystroke `uncertain`; clients
cannot tell it from other uncertain outcomes. This task adds an optional
`reason` and maps `stray_keystroke` to "A stray keystroke may be in the
input box" (P1 follow-ups; spec §6.2 "After a keystroke").

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/acd-protocol/src/model.rs`, `src/messages.rs`,
  `PROTOCOL.md`, `schema/server.schema.json` (regenerated)
- Create: `crates/acd-protocol/examples/server/request.resolved.stray.json`
- Modify: `crates/acd-protocol/tests/messages.rs`, `tests/model.rs`
- Modify: `crates/acd-core/src/registry.rs`, `src/log.rs`,
  `tests/claims.rs`, `tests/registry.rs` (struct literals)
- Modify: `crates/acd-client/src/session.rs`, `src/conn.rs`,
  `src/inbox.rs`, `src/view.rs`
- Modify: `app/src/lib/bindings/*.ts` (regenerated)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2's registry.
- Produces:
  - `acd_protocol::ResolveReason { StrayKeystroke, Unknown }`
    (`#[serde(other)]` on `Unknown`), serialized `"stray_keystroke"`.
  - `ServerMessage::RequestResolved { …, reason: Option<ResolveReason> }`
    (`default`, `skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none"`).
  - `Change::RequestResolved { …, reason: Option<ResolveReason> }`.
  - `Observation::RequestEndedWithReason { request_id: String, by: ResolvedBy, reason: ResolveReason }`.
  - `Registry::resolve_request_with(&mut self, request_id: &str, by: ResolvedBy, reason: Option<ResolveReason>) -> Vec<Change>`;
    `resolve_request` calls it with `None`.
  - `acd_client::view::resolution_notice(by: ResolvedBy, reason: Option<ResolveReason>) -> Option<Notice>`.

- `view.rs`:

```rust
pub const NOTICE_STRAY_KEYSTROKE: &str = "A stray keystroke may be in the input box";

pub fn resolution_notice(by: ResolvedBy, reason: Option<ResolveReason>) -> Option<Notice> {
    if by == ResolvedBy::Uncertain && reason == Some(ResolveReason::StrayKeystroke) {
        return Some(Notice { tone: NoticeTone::Warning, text: NOTICE_STRAY_KEYSTROKE.into() });
    }
    match by {
        ResolvedBy::Terminal => Some(Notice { tone: NoticeTone::Info, text: "Answered in the terminal".into() }),
        ResolvedBy::Uncertain | ResolvedBy::Lost | ResolvedBy::Unknown => Some(Notice {
            tone: NoticeTone::Warning,
            text: NOTICE_CHECK_TERMINAL.into(),
        }),
        ResolvedBy::Inbox | ResolvedBy::Agent | ResolvedBy::Superseded => None,
    }
}
```

### Task 4: herdr keys: set-once targets, per-agent delivery lock, stray reason

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/acd-adapters/src/herdr/keys.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Observation::RequestEndedWithReason`, `ResolveReason` (Task 3).
- Produces:
  - `KeysTargets::set(&self, t: KeysTarget) -> Result<(), &'static str>`:
    first registration wins; an identical re-set is `Ok`; a different
    target for a live or retired request id is `Err`.
  - `herdr::keys::AgentLocks` with
    `pub async fn lock(&self, agent_id: &str) -> tokio::sync::OwnedMutexGuard<()>`.
  - `HerdrAdapter::agent_locks(&self) -> Arc<AgentLocks>`.
  - The keys engine resolves a stray with
    `RequestEndedWithReason { by: Uncertain, reason: StrayKeystroke }`.

In `keys.rs` `mod tests`:

```rust
    #[test]
    fn a_target_is_set_once_per_request_id() {
        let (t, dialog) = claude_target();
        let targets = KeysTargets::default();
        targets.set(t.clone()).unwrap();
        targets.set(t.clone()).unwrap(); // identical: no-op
        let redrawn = dialog.replace("⏺ Creating empty file", "⏺ Creating an empty file");
        let other = KeysTarget::from_screen(
            "r1".into(), "a".into(), CorrelationKey("k".into()), &redrawn,
            [("allow".to_string(), "1".to_string())].into(),
        ).unwrap();
        assert!(targets.set(other.clone()).is_err(), "a different target never replaces");
        assert_eq!(targets.get("r1").unwrap().fingerprint(), t.fingerprint());
        targets.remove("r1");
        assert!(targets.set(other).is_err(), "a retired request id never gets a target again");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn agent_locks_serialize_per_agent_and_forget_idle_entries() {
        let locks = Arc::new(AgentLocks::default());
        let g = locks.lock("a").await;
        let l2 = locks.clone();
        let waiter = tokio::spawn(async move { l2.lock("a").await; });
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50)).await;
        assert!(!waiter.is_finished(), "a second lock on the same agent waits");
        let _other = locks.lock("b").await; // another agent does not wait
        drop(g);
        waiter.await.unwrap();
        drop(_other);
        let _ = locks.lock("c").await;
        assert!(locks.len() <= 1, "idle entries are pruned");
    }
```

In `crates/acd-adapters/tests/herdr_adapter.rs`, replace

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_target_cannot_be_replaced_mid_delivery() {
    let (r, rid) = keys_rig("claude-idle-detection.txt", "claude-idle-prompt.txt").await;
    let gate = r.fake.block_next("agent.read");
    let pending = tokio::spawn({
        let core = r.core.clone();
        let rid = rid.clone();
        async move {
            core.handle("c", ClientMessage::RequestAnswer {
                id: "a1".into(), request_id: rid, revision: None,
                option_id: Some("allow".into()), text: None,
            }).await
        }
    });
    gate.arrived().await;
    let old = r.adapter.keys_targets().get(&rid).unwrap();
    let replaced = KeysTarget::from_screen(
        rid.clone(), old.agent_id().to_string(), old.key().clone(), &redrawn_dialog(), allow_deny(),
    ).unwrap();
    assert!(r.adapter.keys_targets().set(replaced).is_err());
    gate.release();
    let res = pending.await.unwrap();
    assert!(delivered(&res), "{res:?}");
    assert_eq!(r.fake.keys_sent().len(), 1, "the original target was used");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_held_agent_lock_delays_the_key() {
    let (r, rid) = keys_rig("claude-idle-detection.txt", "claude-idle-prompt.txt").await;
    let agent_id = r.core.request(&rid).unwrap().agent_id;
    let guard = r.adapter.agent_locks().lock(&agent_id).await;
    let pending = tokio::spawn({
        let core = r.core.clone();
        let rid = rid.clone();
        async move {
            core.handle("c", ClientMessage::RequestAnswer {
                id: "a1".into(), request_id: rid, revision: None,
                option_id: Some("allow".into()), text: None,
            }).await
        }
    });
    tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(300)).await;
    assert!(r.fake.keys_sent().is_empty(), "no key while another path holds the agent");
    // The other path opens a different request meanwhile: the claim loses.
    let mut other = permission_obs(&agent_id, "k2");
    other.answer_path = AnswerPath::Keys;
    r.core.observe(Observation::RequestSeen(other));
    drop(guard);
    let res = pending.await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(res.error_code(), Some(ErrorCode::RequestGone), "{res:?}");
    assert!(r.fake.keys_sent().is_empty());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn stray_keystroke_carries_its_reason() {
    let (r, rid) = keys_rig("claude-idle-detection.txt", "claude-idle-prompt-stray-1.txt").await;
    r.adapter.set_evidence(Arc::new(Fixed(Verdict::Consistent)));
    let (_, mut rx) = r.core.attach(None);
    answer(&r, "a1", &rid, "allow").await;
    loop {
        if let Ok(ServerMessage::RequestResolved { request_id, by, reason, .. }) = rx.recv().await
            && request_id == rid
        {
            assert_eq!((by, reason), (ResolvedBy::Uncertain, Some(ResolveReason::StrayKeystroke)));
            break;
        }
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement set-once targets**

Replace the `KeysTargets` storage:

```rust
/// Retired request ids remembered so a removed target is never re-set.
const RETIRED_CAP: usize = 4096;

#[derive(Default)]
struct TargetsInner {
    live: HashMap<String, KeysTarget>,
    retired: std::collections::VecDeque<String>,
    retired_set: std::collections::HashSet<String>,
}

/// Registered targets, by request id. A target is immutable for its
/// request id (D8): the content the user reviewed is the content the
/// guard checks.
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct KeysTargets(Mutex<TargetsInner>);

impl TargetsInner {
    fn retire(&mut self, request_id: &str) {
        if self.retired_set.insert(request_id.to_owned()) {
            self.retired.push_back(request_id.to_owned());
            if self.retired.len() > RETIRED_CAP
                && let Some(old) = self.retired.pop_front()
            {
                self.retired_set.remove(&old);
            }
        }
    }
}

impl KeysTarget {
    fn same_as(&self, o: &KeysTarget) -> bool {
        self.agent_id == o.agent_id
            && self.key == o.key
            && self.digest == o.digest
            && self.identity == o.identity
            && self.option_keys == o.option_keys
    }
}
```

and rewrite the methods over `TargetsInner` (`lock()` returns
`MutexGuard<'_, TargetsInner>`):

```rust
    pub fn set(&self, t: KeysTarget) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
        check_keys(&t.option_keys)?;
        let mut inner = self.lock();
        if inner.retired_set.contains(&t.request_id) {
            return Err("this request's target was already retired");
        }
        match inner.live.get(&t.request_id) {
            Some(old) if old.same_as(&t) => Ok(()),
            Some(_) => Err("a different target is already registered for this request"),
            None => {
                inner.live.insert(t.request_id.clone(), t);
                Ok(())
            }
        }
    }

    pub fn get(&self, request_id: &str) -> Option<KeysTarget> {
        self.lock().live.get(request_id).cloned()
    }

    pub fn remove(&self, request_id: &str) -> Option<KeysTarget> {
        let mut inner = self.lock();
        inner.retire(request_id);
        inner.live.remove(request_id)
    }

    pub fn for_agent(&self, agent_id: &str) -> Vec<KeysTarget> {
        self.lock().live.values().filter(|t| t.agent_id == agent_id).cloned().collect()
    }

    pub(crate) fn all(&self) -> Vec<KeysTarget> {
        self.lock().live.values().cloned().collect()
    }

    pub(crate) fn remove_agent(&self, agent_id: &str) {
        let mut inner = self.lock();
        let ids: Vec<String> = inner.live.values().filter(|t| t.agent_id == agent_id).map(|t| t.request_id.clone()).collect();
        for id in ids {
            inner.retire(&id);
            inner.live.remove(&id);
        }
    }
```

Update the doc comment of `set` ("Registers or replaces…") to describe
the set-once rule.

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement the agent locks**

```rust
/// One async lock per agent (D7). The engine holds it from just before
/// its lease check until the key write completes. P3 holds it around any
/// observation that opens a structured request on a herdr agent, switches
/// a request to `keys`, or migrates a request into a herdr agent, so a
/// lease check can never pass on a state P3 is about to change.
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct AgentLocks(Mutex<HashMap<String, Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<()>>>>);

impl AgentLocks {
    pub async fn lock(&self, agent_id: &str) -> tokio::sync::OwnedMutexGuard<()> {
        let m = {
            let mut map = self.0.lock().expect("agent locks");
            // An entry nobody holds or waits for is only a clone in the map.
            map.retain(|_, m| Arc::strong_count(m) > 1);
            map.entry(agent_id.to_owned()).or_default().clone()
        };
        m.lock_owned().await
    }

    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.0.lock().expect("agent locks").len()
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.len() == 0
    }
}
```

`Engine` gains `locks: Arc<AgentLocks>` (created in `Engine::new`) and
`pub(crate) fn locks(&self) -> Arc<AgentLocks>`. In `Engine::deliver`,
between `connect_until` and the lease check:

```rust
        let lock = match timeout_at(deadline, self.locks.lock(&request.agent_id)).await {
            Ok(g) => g,
            Err(_) => return Err(not_delivered(ErrorCode::SourceUnavailable)),
        };
        if !self.lease_held(&core, &target, option_id) {
            drop(lock);
            drop(connected); // unused: nothing was sent
            return Err(not_delivered(ErrorCode::RequestGone));
        }
```

and release it right after the write:

```rust
        let written = connected
            .write("agent.send_keys", json!({"target": pane.pane_id, "keys": [key]}))
            .await
            .map_err(|_| not_delivered(ErrorCode::SourceUnavailable))?;
        drop(lock); // the key is out; herdr's reply wait needs no lock
```

```rust
    }
```

```rust
        let (by, reason) = if seen.stray {
```

```rust
    let mut removed = false;
    loop {
        match rx.recv().await.unwrap() {
            ServerMessage::RequestResolved { request_id, by, .. } if request_id == rid => assert_eq!(by, ResolvedBy::Lost),
            ServerMessage::AgentRemoved { agent_id, .. } if agent_id == id => removed = true,
            ServerMessage::AgentUpdated { agent, .. } if agent.id == id && removed => {

async fn a_changed_session_is_a_new_agent_but_a_first_session_is_not() {
    let r = rig(&[("w1:p1", "claude", "idle")]).await;
    let id = agent(&r).id;
    let (_, mut rx) = r.core.attach(None);
    let mut info = fake_agent("w1:p1", "claude", "idle");
    info["agent_session"] = serde_json::json!({"value": "s-1"});
    r.fake.set_agent(info.clone());
    r.fake.push_event(serde_json::json!({"event": "pane_updated", "data": {"pane_id": "w1:p1"}}));
    until("session known", || r.adapter.panes().read().unwrap().get(&id).and_then(|p| p.session.clone())).await;
    info["agent_session"] = serde_json::json!({"value": "s-2"});
    r.fake.set_agent(info);
    r.fake.push_event(serde_json::json!({"event": "pane_updated", "data": {"pane_id": "w1:p1"}}));
    let mut removals = 0;
    while let Ok(Ok(m)) = tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(2), rx.recv()).await {
        if matches!(m, ServerMessage::AgentRemoved { ref agent_id, .. } if *agent_id == id) {
            removals += 1;
        }
        if r.adapter.panes().read().unwrap().get(&id).and_then(|p| p.session.clone()).as_deref() == Some("s-2") { break; }
    }
    assert_eq!(removals, 1, "null -> s-1 is not a new agent; s-1 -> s-2 is");
```

```rust
    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_claim_after_the_status_read_keeps_the_target() {

        let core = Core::new(test_machine(), vec![fake.clone()]);

                id: "x".into(), request_id: r2, revision: None, option_id: Some("allow".into()), text: None }).await
        });

        assert!(!retire_target(&core, &keys, &rid, Some(RequestStatus::Open)));

        fake.open_gate();
        pending.await.unwrap();

        assert!(retire_target(&core, &keys, &rid, Some(RequestStatus::Open)));
        assert!(core.request(&rid).is_none());
        assert!(keys.get(&rid).is_none());
    }
}
```

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct PaneIdentity {
    terminal_id: Option<String>,
    session: Option<String>,
    kind: AgentKind,
}

impl PaneIdentity {
    /// A different terminal, a different reported session, or a different
    /// agent kind means a new agent under the same pane id. A session that
    /// appears for the first time does not.
    fn replaced_by(&self, next: &PaneIdentity) -> bool {
        self.kind != next.kind
            || matches!((&self.terminal_id, &next.terminal_id), (Some(a), Some(b)) if a != b)
            || matches!((&self.session, &next.session), (Some(a), Some(b)) if a != b)
    }
}
```


Rewrite `apply`, factoring the old `None` branch into `forget_pane`:

```rust
    fn apply(&mut self, pane_id: &str, info: Option<AgentInfo>) {
        let agent_id = map::agent_id(&self.hash, pane_id);
        let Some((info, obs)) = info.and_then(|i| map::agent_obs(&self.hash, &i).map(|o| (i, o))) else {
            self.closed.remove(pane_id);
            self.identity.remove(pane_id);
            self.forget_pane(pane_id);
            return;
        };
        let next = PaneIdentity {
            terminal_id: info.terminal_id.clone(),
            session: info.agent_session.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.value.clone()),
            kind: obs.kind,
        };
        let closed = self.closed.remove(pane_id);
        let replaced = self.identity.get(pane_id).is_some_and(|old| old.replaced_by(&next));
        if self.known.contains_key(pane_id) && (closed || replaced) {
            self.forget_pane(pane_id);
        }
        self.identity.insert(pane_id.to_owned(), next.clone());
        if self.known.insert(pane_id.to_owned(), obs.kind) != Some(obs.kind) {
            self.subs_changed = true;
        }
        if obs.state == AgentState::NeedsYou {
            self.blocked.insert(pane_id.to_owned());
        } else {
            self.blocked.remove(pane_id);
        }
        self.panes.write().expect("pane map").insert(
            agent_id,
            PaneRef {
                herdr: self.herdr.clone(),
                pane_id: pane_id.to_owned(),
                kind: obs.kind,
                hints: self.hints_tx.clone(),
                socket: self.herdr.path().to_path_buf(),
                session: next.session,
                cwd: info.foreground_cwd.clone().or_else(|| info.cwd.clone()),
                terminal_id: next.terminal_id,
            },
        );
        self.fallback.on_state(pane_id, obs.state == AgentState::NeedsYou, Instant::now().into_std());
        self.core.observe(Observation::AgentUpsert(obs));
    }

    /// The pane's agent is gone: drop everything known about it.
    fn forget_pane(&mut self, pane_id: &str) {
        let agent_id = map::agent_id(&self.hash, pane_id);
        self.fallback.forget(pane_id);
        self.fb_open.remove(pane_id);
        self.keys.remove_agent(&agent_id);
        if self.known.remove(pane_id).is_some() {
            self.subs_changed = true;
            self.blocked.remove(pane_id);
            self.panes.write().expect("pane map").remove(&agent_id);
            self.core.observe(Observation::AgentGone { agent_id });
        }
    }
```

A `closed` entry for a pane that is never re-fetched is cleared by the
next `apply` of that pane or by `lost()` (add `self.closed.clear()` and
keep `identity` there, since panes survive a reconnect).

Replace the tail of `watch_targets` (from `self.watch_absent.remove`)
with a call to a free function, so the ordering is unit-testable:

```rust
            if retire_target(&self.core, &self.keys, &t.request_id, status) {
                self.watch_absent.remove(&t.request_id);
                self.mark_dirty(pane_id.to_owned());
            } else {
                // A claim landed after the status read. Its guard will see
                // the dialog gone and release the request; the next absent
                // read ends it.
                self.watch_absent.insert(t.request_id.clone(), ABSENT_READS - 1);

```

```rust
/// The dialog of `request_id`'s target is confirmed gone. Ends an open
/// request `terminal` first, then removes the target, unless a claim got
/// in between (P1 follow-up: removing first left a claimed request with
/// no target and no watcher). Returns whether the target was removed.
pub fn retire_target(
    core: &Core,
    keys: &KeysTargets,
    request_id: &str,
    status_at_read: Option<RequestStatus>,
) -> bool {
    if status_at_read == Some(RequestStatus::Open) {
        core.observe(Observation::RequestEndedIfOpen {
            request_id: request_id.to_owned(),
            by: ResolvedBy::Terminal,
        });
    }
    if core.request(request_id).map(|r| r.status) == Some(RequestStatus::Answering) {
        return false;
    }
    keys.remove(request_id);
    core.observe(Observation::DialogGoneById { request_id: request_id.to_owned() });
    true
}
```

  - `wire::Payload { session_id: String, prompt_id: Option<String>, turn_id: Option<String>, cwd: Option<String>, tool_name: Option<String>, tool_input: Value, tool_response: Value, permission_suggestions: Vec<Value>, last_assistant_message: Option<String>, agent_id: Option<String> }`,
    `Payload::from_value(&Value) -> Option<Payload>`, `Payload::is_subagent(&self) -> bool`.
  - `wire::Hints { herdr_pane: Option<String>, herdr_socket: Option<String>, entrypoint: Option<String> }`,
    `Hints::from_headers(&http::HeaderMap) -> Hints`, `wire::hint(Option<&str>) -> Option<String>`.
  - `wire::HookEvent { agent: HookAgent, name: HookName, payload: Payload, hints: Hints, at: tokio::time::Instant }`.
  - `wire::valid_id(&str) -> bool`, `wire::canonical_json(&Value) -> String`,
    `wire::tool_digest(&str, &Value) -> String`, `wire::command_of(&Value) -> Option<&str>`,
    `wire::claude_key(&Payload) -> Option<CorrelationKey>`, `wire::codex_key(&Payload) -> Option<CorrelationKey>`.
  - `wire::AlwaysRule { tool_name: String, rule_content: String }`,
    `wire::always_rule(&[Value]) -> Option<AlwaysRule>`,
    `wire::reply_allow() -> String`, `reply_deny() -> String`, `reply_allow_always(&AlwaysRule) -> String`.
  - `mapping::detail_of(input: &Value) -> String` and `mapping::DETAIL_CHARS = 4000`.
  - `mapping::claude_permission(agent_id: &str, key: CorrelationKey, upgrade_from: Option<CorrelationKey>, p: &Payload) -> RequestObs`.
  - `mapping::claude_keys_options() -> Vec<RequestOption>` and
    `mapping::claude_permission_keys(dialog: &herdr::screen::Dialog) -> HashMap<String, String>`.
  - `mapping::claude_question(agent_id: &str, key: CorrelationKey, upgrade_from: Option<CorrelationKey>, p: &Payload, bound: bool) -> Option<RequestObs>`,
    `mapping::question_keys(n: usize) -> HashMap<String, String>`.
  - `mapping::codex_permission(agent_id: &str, key: CorrelationKey, upgrade_from: Option<CorrelationKey>, p: &Payload, collapsed: bool) -> RequestObs`,
    `mapping::CODEX_KEYS: [(&str, &str); 3]`.
  - `mapping::payload_for_log(raw: &[u8]) -> String` and
    `mapping::log_payload(agent: HookAgent, name: &HookName, raw: &[u8])`.

```rust
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn fx(rel: &str) -> Value {

        serde_json::from_str(&std::fs::read_to_string(p).unwrap()).unwrap()

    #[test]

            "claude/payload-PermissionRequest-Bash.json", "claude/payload-PermissionRequest-AskUserQuestion.json",
            "claude/payload-PreToolUse-AskUserQuestion.json", "claude/payload-PostToolUse-Bash.json",
            "claude/payload-PostToolUse-AskUserQuestion.json", "claude/payload-Stop.json",
            "claude/payload-Stop-after-deny.json", "claude/payload-UserPromptSubmit.json",
            "claude/payload-SessionStart.json", "claude/payload-Notification.json",
            "codex/payload-PermissionRequest-Bash.json", "codex/payload-PostToolUse-Bash.json",
            "codex/payload-SessionStart.json", "codex/payload-UserPromptSubmit.json",
        ] {
            let p = payload(rel);
            assert!(valid_id(&p.session_id), "{rel}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_or_hostile_ids_are_rejected() {
        assert!(Payload::from_value(&serde_json::json!({"cwd": "/x"})).is_none(), "no session id");
        assert!(Payload::from_value(&serde_json::json!([1, 2])).is_none(), "not an object");
        let long = "a".repeat(129);
        assert!(Payload::from_value(&serde_json::json!({"session_id": long})).is_none());
        assert!(Payload::from_value(&serde_json::json!({"session_id": "a\u{7}b"})).is_none());
        let p = Payload::from_value(&serde_json::json!({"session_id": "s", "prompt_id": 5, "permission_suggestions": {"x": 1}})).unwrap();
        assert_eq!((p.prompt_id, p.permission_suggestions.len()), (None, 0), "wrong types are absent, not fatal");
    }

    #[test]
    fn same_turn_different_tools_get_different_keys() {
        let a = payload("claude/payload-PermissionRequest-Bash.json");
        let mut b = a.clone();
        b.tool_input["command"] = "touch /tmp/acp0/other.txt".into();
        assert_eq!(a.prompt_id, b.prompt_id, "prompt_id is per turn (D2)");
        assert_ne!(claude_key(&a), claude_key(&b));
        let mut c = a.clone();
        c.tool_input = serde_json::from_str(r#"{"description":"Create empty file","command":"touch /tmp/acp0/t1.txt"}"#).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(claude_key(&a), claude_key(&c), "key order does not matter");
    }

    #[test]
    fn question_pre_tool_use_and_permission_request_share_a_key() {
        let pre = payload("claude/payload-PreToolUse-AskUserQuestion.json");
        let perm = payload("claude/payload-PermissionRequest-AskUserQuestion.json");
        assert_eq!(claude_key(&pre), claude_key(&perm));
        assert!(claude_key(&pre).unwrap().0.starts_with("claude:4e3885ee-"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn codex_post_tool_use_matches_by_command_not_digest() {
        let req = payload("codex/payload-PermissionRequest-Bash.json");
        let post = payload("codex/payload-PostToolUse-Bash.json");
        assert_ne!(tool_digest("Bash", &req.tool_input), tool_digest("Bash", &post.tool_input), "description is dropped (D3)");
        assert_eq!(command_of(&req.tool_input), command_of(&post.tool_input));
        assert!(codex_key(&req).unwrap().0.starts_with("codex:01a116d3-"));
        assert!(codex_key(&post).is_some());

        let v = |s: String| serde_json::from_str::<Value>(&s).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v(reply_allow()), fx("claude/response-allow.json"));
        assert_eq!(v(reply_deny()), fx("claude/response-deny.json"));
        let rule = AlwaysRule { tool_name: "Bash".into(), rule_content: "touch /tmp/acp0/*".into() };
        assert_eq!(v(reply_allow_always(&rule)), fx("claude/response-allow-always-localSettings.json"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn always_rule_needs_an_add_rules_suggestion() {
        let p = payload("claude/payload-PermissionRequest-Bash.json");
        assert_eq!(always_rule(&p.permission_suggestions), None, "addDirectories and setMode are not rules");
        let s = serde_json::json!([{"type": "addRules", "rules": [{"toolName": "Bash", "ruleContent": "npm test"}],
                                    "behavior": "allow", "destination": "session"}]);
        assert_eq!(always_rule(s.as_array().unwrap()),
                   Some(AlwaysRule { tool_name: "Bash".into(), rule_content: "npm test".into() }));
    }

    #[test]
    fn hints_are_absent_when_empty_long_or_controlled() {
        assert_eq!(hint(Some("")), None, "an unlisted env var interpolates to \"\" (gate 2a)");
        assert_eq!(hint(Some(&"x".repeat(1025))), None);
        assert_eq!(hint(Some("w1:p2\t")), None);
        assert_eq!(hint(Some("w1:p2")).as_deref(), Some("w1:p2"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn names_parse_and_unknown_is_other() {
        assert_eq!(HookName::parse("PermissionRequest"), HookName::PermissionRequest);
        assert_eq!(HookName::parse("Notification"), HookName::Other);
        assert_eq!(HookAgent::parse("codex"), Some(HookAgent::Codex));
        assert_eq!(HookAgent::parse("aider"), None);
    }
}
```
